# OR-09 — Make the go-or-wait decision on a retained throw

**Separate instructional session · Week 2, Day 4 · individually authored · written athletic design reviewed**

Independent source, written programming and numeric reviews passed. The companion audit covers 7,776 timing scenarios, 216 mixed triad patterns and 52 rejecting probes. Current canonical release, actual facilities/athlete histories and precise separate tumbling remain unverified.

## Daily stimulus brief

Today teaches one early THROW/HOLD decision on a genuinely retained stationary chest projection. Full preparation rehearses the owned arm path and handling. The first existing main opportunity is a counted known-cue E0 rehearsal with complete retrieval; E1 uses only the remaining actual opportunity prefix for choice learning. Push/pull go first in Strength, followed by familiar knee, hip and brace work. Keep actual ball, direction, intent, strength setup and recent smaller doses. Attention and equipment conduct are the instructional targets; no reaction-time improvement or harder throwing is promised.

**Success:** On an individually addressed early cue, the athlete either uses the owned release or keeps the ball, parks it and waits. Record the actual cue, response, movement quality and retrieval conduct separately. A first choice can be useful learning without proving repeatability or both rules. No percentage quota, distance target score or speed contest is used.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-05/direct stable throw and known-release/retrieval, plus the most recent OR-08/direct support-dose and response record → known E0 rehearsal and only remaining OR-09 choices → OR-13 applies its separately qualified cue/movement branch → OR-17 observes that actual retained branch with reduced prompting. Dates and missing attendance do not manufacture history.

## Athlete and delivery assumptions

Separate instructional on-ramp; 15 athletes, three lanes and two qualified coaches are planning assumptions. Ages 12–14 are the reference, not a novice label. All three age groups receive explicit doses and the same task-specific entry decisions; older athletes do not automatically receive a larger ball or more choices. Directly competent athletes may use their separately qualified main branch instead of unnecessary remedial attendance.

Before the new choice, require repeatable actual same-demand stationary throwing, comfortable arm path, secure ball/chest handling, actual floor pickup/carry/set-down, understood and repeatable known-cue/retrieval behavior, fitting rack access and current suitable response. Confirm both signal meanings verbally, with no extra athlete practice release. The current E0 rehearsal must remain suitable before E1. Prior whole go/no-go mastery is not needed for its first counted instruction. An emerging throw or new implement uses known-cue instruction instead. Each no-release alternative and strength role retains independent gates. Pain, distress, restrictions, uncontained ball, unsafe traffic or loss of control ends the affected task; no additional test secures clearance.

PLANNING ONLY: 15 athletes, 2 qualified coaches, 3 parallel throw lanes, each 3 m wide × 6 m protected forward length, with additional 1 m side buffer between lanes, a rear queue/set-up area and unobstructed coach access. No athlete waits in a target lane. A broad optional first-landing reference band 1–3 m ahead helps explain forward direction; it is not a distance requirement, floor slam target or score. The ball follows a natural forward arc and lands by gravity. Lane perimeter/soft containment must stop the actual ball without hazardous rebound, crossing into a neighbor, or contact with a person; six metres is a planning test, not a guaranteed containment distance. Before use the coaches inspect actual ball behavior and the complete retrieval route. If containment is not verified, use a no-release task, not an improvised wall or partner throw. Three intact soft low-rebound medicine balls, one per active lane, and three stable racks at comfortable hand height for E1 are required; P2 uses three marked floor-ball spots with a clear 1 m out-and-back carrying space behind the throw line; size/type/mass may differ by actual athlete fit. Reference 0.5 kg / 18 cm is a proposed equipment test point, not a uniform mandatory load. Actual values must be recorded and replacements inspected; no automatic heavier age tier. The standardized preparation retains 15 personal 3 × 3 m bays. Strength requires five suitable high pressing surfaces (90 cm reference only), five suitable benches and five manageable dumbbells for rows, five mats; supports can be reused sequentially if each purpose fits and the transition is verified. Breathing alternative needs five lower-leg supports; suspension alternative needs five independently verified secure anchors. Quantity, dimensions, sightlines and equipment fit remain unverified. For this reference clock, each lane’s declared ball and fixed rack must fit every assigned athlete who uses it; ball/rack changes are not hidden inside a zero-time handover. If actual changes or slower instruction are required, delay/omit later work within the component or select an independently eligible no-release task. The retained familiar DB hip option additionally needs five fitting elevated stations and ten simultaneously suitable DBs; two coaches observe two/three independently familiar athletes. Between-wave handling reset has its own 20 s allowance. Actual inventory, each assigned athlete’s fit and combined bay/lane sightlines must be verified.

Keep athlete order 0–14. P1 has three groups of five in personal bays; P2 has five triads. Main actions are sequential within each triad: one addressed athlete at a time on15 s slots. Others keep balls parked and wait behind their own line. Both coaches can observe the active cue; one then watches released-ball owners while the other watches rear queues/racks. HOLD/no-release athletes do not retrieve. All lanes close before collection, and an immediate fault cancels later actions until safe. Stationary alternatives stay in visible personal bays; no imaginary return or unseen transfer. Strength has three waves of five in each sequential role, observed two/three per coach. Actual one-to-one teaching needs reduce or defer later work.

All demonstrations, readiness discussion, ball/rack setup, actions, shutdown, retrieval, water and transitions stay inside the stated windows. Release times are earliest starts; actual slower handling or a fault delays/omits later work. Do not force pace, shorten required recovery, remove the full base or add opportunities to fill an empty slot.

## Complete preparation

Use the unchanged instructional standard/compact base fully resolved below. The two targets use40 s P1 unloaded position and140 s P2 no-release floor handling. The explicit OR-09 amendment places one complete familiar throw rehearsal in counted E0 within the existing main opportunity ceiling. Neither preparation task throws or supplies a cue/throw pass.

**Preparation layout and delivery assumptions:**

athletes: 15; athletic coaches: 2; lanes: 3; athletes assigned per lane: 5; layout: Three columns of five distinct 3 m × 3 m personal bays: 15 independent working spaces. Five athletes share a lane assignment, never an overlapping crawl corridor.; minimum marked rectangle m: width: 9; length: 15; additional space: Unobstructed peripheral coach circulation/access/egress and separately verified start, runoff and return corridors are additional to the 9 m × 15 m bay rectangle. The marked rectangle is not the total facility footprint.; travel: Standing paths remain in personal bays. Short forward passes are at most 1.6 m; backward/lateral spans 1 m or less except 1.5 m carioca. Individually check full hand-walk/plank footprint before use.; orientation: All bays face one end. Complete a pass and stop before ordinary walking return within that same bay. No crossing between athletes or concurrent opposite-direction traffic inside a bay.; coaches: Familiar shared practice: one coach scans A/B and one scans B/C with clear oblique views. Three-wave teaching/checks: both coaches watch one active lane and split five athletes 2/3; other lanes stand. Clear views of all active feet/trunks are required.; wave order: lane_a; lane_b; lane_c; wave handover: The active lane finishes standing, then the next lane starts on cue. The 2-second change (3 seconds for floor teaching) is a cue change, not relocation. Athletes stay in their own bays.; equipment: Flat markers, bottles outside active rectangles, clean dry nonslip surface. No partner support, unstable props or unverified anchor is assumed.; setup before clock: Mark/inspect space before class. Readiness/restriction information must be available. First demonstration includes stop cue and immediate comfort check. Unknown restrictions or inability to follow safe directions require individual resolution, never assumed clearance.; clock definition: Row time = demonstration + waves × individual execution envelope + explicit wave handovers + reset/transition. The individual envelope includes stated steps, pauses and returns. Shared practice means separate bays, not 15 times one athlete dose.; tempo rule: Execution envelopes are estimates for the stated variant, not speed commands. If safe learning/movement needs longer, use contingency, remove optional work or revise the booking. Never rush to validate arithmetic.; bay to target transition: Base ends at 12:00 full or 7:00 compact with athletes standing in bays. Reserve exactly 180 seconds for exactly two parent-owned target tasks. The parent exemplar defines the split and charges bay-to-start gathering, demonstrations, observed holds, rehearsal, exit and return within those 180 seconds. Use the parent-defined allocation; do not double-charge gathering or hide it before the base ends.; physical validation status: UNVERIFIED: measured space, body fit, sightlines, surface, shared-floor schedule and target corridors have not been observed. Arithmetic does not certify physical feasibility.

Use one base profile for the booking. The instructional profile replaces the familiar base when needed; it is never added to it. L changes the training prescriptions below, while the selected preparation stays easy.

**Exact execution and counting for this profile:**

**SP-01, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Easy walking march (1 × 60 s). Tempo: Natural walking rhythm, without a technical high-knee target. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: Time determines the dose; no repetition or contact count is predetermined. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-02, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Two backward and two forward steps (4 × 2 s) → Settle (1 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 seconds per step; no rapid backpedal. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One cycle is two backward steps followed by two forward return steps. Per-side values count backward steps only. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-03, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Rightward step-close (1 × 4 s) → Leftward step-close (1 × 4 s) → Settle (1 × 2 s). Tempo: Slow walking steps; 4 seconds each direction. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One cycle is one rightward traversal and one leftward traversal; traversal counts are not footstep counts. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-05, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Shallow forward-step bend and return (2 × 6 s). Tempo: Step 1 s; small bend 2; rise 1; return/reset 2. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete lunge/shift and return on one working side is one repetition; left plus right equals reps_total. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-06, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Shallow lateral shift and return (2 × 6 s). Tempo: Step 1 s; small shift 2; comfortable pause 1; return/reset 2. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete lunge/shift and return on one working side is one repetition; left plus right equals reps_total. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-07, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Kneel and place hands comfortably (1 × 8 s) → Gentle rock-back and return with knees down (2 × 3 s) → Return through kneeling to standing and settle (1 × 10 s). Tempo: Rock back 1 s, pause 1, return 1. No hover, plank or inverted stretch. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Coach observes comfortable hand/knee support and return; if unavailable, omit floor action and record incomplete purpose. Counting: Count only completed hip rock-back/return cycles; kneeling entry and standing exit are separate transitions. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-08, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Small slow descent, stick, stand (2 × 5 s). Tempo: Lower 2 s; hold 2; stand 1. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One lowering, stable hold and return to standing is one sequence. Familiar walking-entry steps are secondary counts, not descent repetitions or landing events. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-09, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Four easy alternating march steps (4 × 2 s) → Settle (1 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 s per easy step; comfortable walking height. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One left or right march step is one primary count. A left-right cycle is two steps and is retained separately as bilateral_march_cycles; walking resets are excluded. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-13, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Four alternating foot-roll and replace actions (4 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 s per gentle roll/load and replace; no forced toe walking. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One gentle roll/load and replace on one foot is one action; this is not a left-right pair. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-15, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Small hinge, pause, stand and reset (3 × 5 s). Tempo: Hips back 2 s; pause 1; stand 1; reset 1. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One whole hinge/pause/stand/reset is one bilateral repetition; both sides participate together, not two separate unilateral reps. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-16, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Thigh-supported hinge, small squat, reach, stand/reset (3 × 6 s). Tempo: Hinge 1 s; small squat 1; comfortable reach 1; stand 1; reset 2. Toe contact/deep squat/full overhead range deferred. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete familiar contact/hinge, squat, LEFT reach-and-return, RIGHT reach-and-return, stand/reset is one bilateral repetition. The declared OR partial sequence is a different action with one simultaneous comfortable partial two-arm reach. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

Omitted slots: SP-04, SP-10, SP-11, SP-12, SP-14. No repetitions are assigned to omitted slots.

**Exact execution and counting for this profile:**

**SP-01, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Easy walking march (1 × 30 s). Tempo: Natural walking rhythm, without a technical high-knee target. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: Time determines the dose; no repetition or contact count is predetermined. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-02, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Two backward and two forward steps (4 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 seconds per step. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One cycle is two backward steps followed by two forward return steps. Per-side values count backward steps only. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-03, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Rightward step-close (1 × 4 s) → Leftward step-close (1 × 4 s). Tempo: Slow walking steps; 4 seconds each direction. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One cycle is one rightward traversal and one leftward traversal; traversal counts are not footstep counts. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-05, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Shallow forward-step bend and return (2 × 6 s). Tempo: Step 1 s; small bend 2; rise 1; return/reset 2. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete lunge/shift and return on one working side is one repetition; left plus right equals reps_total. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-06, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Shallow lateral shift and return (2 × 6 s). Tempo: Step 1 s; small shift 2; comfortable pause 1; return/reset 2. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete lunge/shift and return on one working side is one repetition; left plus right equals reps_total. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-07, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Kneel and place hands comfortably (1 × 5 s) → Gentle rock-back and return (1 × 4 s) → Return to standing (1 × 5 s). Tempo: Rock back 1 s, pause 2, return 1; no hover/plank. Use contingency if entry/exit needs longer. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Coach observes comfortable kneeling, hand support and return; no assumed clearance. Counting: Count only completed hip rock-back/return cycles; kneeling entry and standing exit are separate transitions. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-08, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Small slow descent, stick, stand (1 × 5 s). Tempo: Lower 2 s; hold 2; stand 1. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One lowering, stable hold and return to standing is one sequence. Familiar walking-entry steps are secondary counts, not descent repetitions or landing events. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-09, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Four easy alternating march steps (4 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 s per easy step; no forced knee height. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One left or right march step is one primary count. A left-right cycle is two steps and is retained separately as bilateral_march_cycles; walking resets are excluded. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-13, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Four alternating foot-roll and replace actions (4 × 2 s). Tempo: 2 s per gentle roll/load and replace; no forced toe walking. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One gentle roll/load and replace on one foot is one action; this is not a left-right pair. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-15, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Small hinge, pause, stand and reset (1 × 5 s). Tempo: Hips back 2 s; pause 1; stand 1; reset 1. One rep is a readiness observation, not competency pass. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One whole hinge/pause/stand/reset is one bilateral repetition; both sides participate together, not two separate unilateral reps. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

**SP-16, ages 9-11, 12-14, 15-18:** Thigh-supported hinge, small squat, reach, stand/reset (1 × 6 s). Tempo: Hinge 1 s; small squat 1; reach 1; stand 1; reset 2. One cycle does not pass competence. Effort: Easy and conversational; no maximal speed, height, stretch or fatigue target. Entry: Comfortable action, clear personal bay and ability to stop on cue. Counting: One complete familiar contact/hinge, squat, LEFT reach-and-return, RIGHT reach-and-return, stand/reset is one bilateral repetition. The declared OR partial sequence is a different action with one simultaneous comfortable partial two-arm reach. Contacts: No intentional flight; ordinary support/steps are not scored plyometric landings.

Omitted slots: SP-04, SP-10, SP-11, SP-12, SP-14. No repetitions are assigned to omitted slots.

Times are earliest releases. Athletes stay in their own bays; waiting waves observe/rest and add no repetitions. Demonstrations for a familiar profile are reminders only. See the layout above for supervision and handover rules.

### Base coaching reference and readiness overrides

Apply each reference only to an included slot and its specified variant.

**SP-01 — Easy jog**

**Setup:** Center of the personal bay; no traveling loop. Bottle outside active rectangle. The demonstration includes a stop-cue rehearsal and immediate comfort check.

**Cues:** Small relaxed steps; speak easily.; Freeze in your own space on cue.

**Common errors:** Racing or high-knee sprinting.; Drifting into another bay.

**Stop:** Cease for pain, dizziness, unusual breathing difficulty, loss of balance or inability to follow the stop cue; coach checks readiness.

**Competency:** observe: Relaxed rhythm, conversational breathing and independent stop without crossing the boundary.; pass scope: Preparation observation only. A completed repetition does not grant a main-family, loaded-strength, flight, throwing, cue-response or tumbling qualification.; record: Log selected profile, actual variant/dose, comfort, response to cue, omissions and not-observed actions. No actual results are invented.

**SP-02 — Backpedal**

**Setup:** Face the same end of the room. Mark 1 m behind the start; two short backward steps reach it, then two forward walking steps return. No turn or crossing.

**Cues:** Short steps under you; look forward.; Check the clear space behind you before moving.

**Common errors:** Long reaching steps and backward lean.; Turning into adjacent space.

**Stop:** Stop for obstacle, balance loss or pain; use ordinary walking return only when clear.

**Competency:** observe: Controls both backward steps and forward return with balance and stop-cue response.; pass scope: Preparation observation only. A completed repetition does not grant a main-family, loaded-strength, flight, throwing, cue-response or tumbling qualification.; record: Log selected profile, actual variant/dose, comfort, response to cue, omissions and not-observed actions. No actual results are invented.

**SP-03 — Lateral shuffle**

**Setup:** Start centered. Step-close without crossing for 1 m right and 1 m left back to start. Repeat only for the familiar dose.

**Cues:** Stay facing forward; step then close.; Own the stop.

**Common errors:** Crossing feet.; Reaching past the bay or racing the return.

**Stop:** Stop if feet collide, boundary control fails or discomfort appears.

**Competency:** observe: Maintains forward orientation and controlled step-close both directions.; pass scope: Preparation observation only. A completed repetition does not grant a main-family, loaded-strength, flight, throwing, cue-response or tumbling qualification.; record: Log selected profile, actual variant/dose, comfort, response to cue, omissions and not-observed actions. No actual results are invented.

**SP-05 — Walking lunge with rotation/reach**

**Setup:** Forward step within a 1 m span, then return after each rep. Reach toward the front knee with a small comfortable trunk turn. First visit uses thigh contact and no rotation.

**Cues:** Use a stable split base; lower only as far as controlled.; Turn gently toward the front leg and return tall.

**Common errors:** Tightrope stance.; Forced depth or twisting over a painful knee.; Losing balance during reach.

**Stop:** Stop for pain or instability; use smaller split-stance bend if comfortable, otherwise omit and record.

**Competency:** observe: Comfortable split position on both sides with stable foot pressure and smooth return.; pass scope: Preparation observation only. **SP-07 — Inchworm → plank → bear crawl → downward dog (ordered slot)**

**Setup:** One athlete per dry nonslip 3 m × 3 m bay. Entire floor footprint fits before use. No shared slow-crawl lane or following crawler. Coach checks hand/knee comfort before support. Familiar bear support uses one limb at a time: left hand, right hand, left foot, right foot. A lift means the whole selected hand or foot briefly clears the floor by about 1–2 cm; it is not merely a weight shift. Fully replant and settle before the next limb.

**Cues:** Hands comfortable; breathe with controlled trunk.; Quiet unhurried transitions; soft knees in downward dog.; Keep the trunk quiet; lift one contact only, then fully replant before the next.; Do not lift opposite hand and foot together.

**Common errors:** Sagging or breath-holding.; Travel outside the bay.; Forced straight knees or shoulder range.; Starting the next lift before the previous contact is fully replanted.; Rotating or sagging the trunk to clear the limb.

**Stop:** Stop for wrist/shoulder pain, slipping, trunk-control loss or inadequate footprint.; Use contingency or record incomplete rather than rushing floor entry.; Omit the familiar bear-lift component if any single-limb lift/replant has not already been mastered; use the entire named instructional floor profile and its coaching clock, or record incomplete purpose. No new bear-lift teaching is assumed inside the familiar reminder.

**Competency:** observe: Comfortable actual support and floor entry/exit. Rock-back does not pass plank/bear or tumbling support. For familiar bear lifts, each selected contact clears briefly, fully replants, and the trunk remains quiet before the next lift.; pass scope: Preparation observation only. A completed repetition does not grant a main-family, loaded-strength, flight, throwing, cue-response or tumbling qualification.; record: Log selected profile, actual variant/dose, comfort, response to cue, omissions and not-observed actions. No actual results are invented.

## Targeted preparation and training prescriptions

### P1 — Rehearse the owned unloaded chest-start arm path (Prepare & Access)

**Mapping:** CHEST-REACH-TEACH; separate quiet-standing option **Set purpose / contribution:** One unloaded reach-and-return rehearses the retained arm path and organized standing without spending a throwing opportunity. Quiet standing replaces it only under its own comfort gate.

**Setup and execution:** Remain in the personal bay. Feet comfortably apart and planted, knees unlocked. Set hands comfortably at chest level for 2 s, reach both arms forward for 2 s, return for 2 s, relax/reset for 4 s. No ball, preload, forced shoulder position or lower-body movement. Use relaxed open hands with palms facing forward and fingers comfortably upward, as if supporting the back/sides of a small ball. Keep wrists comfortable and elbows below shoulder height without pinning them to the ribs. Reach only through a comfortable owned range; record any adapted orientation.

**Cues:** Feet still; comfortable chest start; reach forward; return and relax. **Common errors:** Arching, shrugging, forcing elbow lock, dipping to create momentum, adding repetitions while watching another group, or treating this as a push-up/row pass.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Keep the same low-demand OR-05 position rehearsal before handling. No new cue choice, throw or upper-body fatigue is added.

**Metadata:** Method: one observed position rehearsal. Tenets: Strength / Body Control; integrated coordination. Pattern: bilateral reach. Equipment: personal bay. Local teaching record, not a released profile.

**standard D**

| Ages 9–11 | Ages 12–14 | Ages 15–18 |
|---|---|---|
| Unloaded Chest-Start Reach and Return — stationary bilateral teaching task; 1 × 1 reps; 10 s/rep; Unloaded, easy 1–2/10; one comfortable bilateral forward reach and return. Both feet stay planted, no dip, arch, step, jump or forced end range.; at least 20 s recovery; One whole counted action per set. Phase times are coaching envelopes, not a demand to rush. P2 never releases a ball; E1 attempts, including faults, use their listed opportunity. No bonus trials. | Unloaded Chest-Start Reach and Return — stationary bilateral teaching task; 1 × 1 reps; 10 s/rep; Unloaded, easy 1–2/10; one comfortable bilateral forward reach and return. **Competency / within-age override:** All ages require comfortable standing and the assigned unloaded reach. A first visitor may learn this single counted action after demonstration. If upper-body reach is unavailable but standing is comfortable, use the explicit 10 s quiet-standing route. That route gives no arm-path evidence. Experienced athletes keep the small dose and actual comfortable range.

**Advance / hold / stop:** Retain one listed action with the actual suitable ball/range where applicable. No added throw, fit trial or second handling attempt. A partial or failed action is counted and may require deferral; changing age is not a progression.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-05/direct arm-path control → one retained OR-09 position rehearsal → counted E0 known task, then only independently eligible E1 cue instruction.

### P2 — Rehearse floor handling and chest position without a release (Prepare & Access)

**Mapping:** BALL-HOLD-TEACH; unloaded/standing options are separate tasks **Set purpose / contribution:** One counted floor pickup, 5 s chest hold, 2 m out-and-back carry, floor set-down and exit rehearses equipment control. It has zero throws and observes no cue inhibition.

**Setup and execution:** Begin with the ball resting on a marked floor spot behind the throw line. After demonstration, use a comfortable bilateral hip/knee bend to pick it up to chest level over 5 s, hold comfortably for 5 s, walk 1 m out and 1 m back in the marked personal handling area over 4 s (2 s walking plus 2 s turning), set the ball back on its original floor spot over 6 s, then exit/reset over 4 s. The ball stays in two hands until set-down. This counted handling loop includes floor access and 2 m loaded carry; no throw, catch, loaded push-out, extra trial, or passing to a child. Do not force a floor reach that is unavailable or hurry the turn; stop the action and let the coach manage the implement if needed. Use both palms/finger pads on the rear-side surfaces of the ball, thumbs comfortably behind it and fingers spread enough for a secure grip; no forced thumb shape or joint angle. Keep wrists comfortable and elbows below shoulder height. Ball rests in the hands at chest level, not squeezed against the throat. Declare the individually fitting contact and retain it across P2/E1; a ball requiring painful wrist position or an insecure spread is unsuitable.

**Cues:** Reach the floor comfortably before taking the ball; two hands; stand still for the hold; walk and turn on instruction; return and set down gently. **Common errors:** Releasing on a setup cue, dropping the ball, prolonged unsupported waiting, forcing grip on a large implement, holding breath, or awarding a throwing/cue-speed pass.

**Selection / order / pairing:** A complete familiar release and whole-group retrieval cannot be assumed to fit the 140 s preparation target. The explicit amendment retains handling here and assigns one counted known-cue rehearsal to E0 within the existing total main opportunity limit.

**Metadata:** Method: observed floor handling and waiting instruction. Tenets: Body Control / Strength; equipment conduct and attention integrated. Pattern: one floor pickup, chest hold, short walking carry/turn and floor set-down. Equipment: declared soft medicine ball, marked original floor spot and a separate unobstructed 1 m out-and-back path per lane; no shared crossing.

**Competency / within-age override:** First-visit handling is taught within this counted set after the coach has selected a plausible comfortable implement by size and prior information. This set provides the actual fit/handling observation; no prior successful ball hold is invented. If grip or position is unsuitable, set down with coach management and end the action; select a separately eligible unloaded or standing route for later work. Record partial handling, not a pass. No more ball trials are added. Comfortable ordinary walking/turning is required for the carry. Comfortable unloaded floor access is independently established or observed during the initial reach of this same counted P2 pickup before accepting the ball load. An owned partial-range hinge/squat does not establish floor access. If that initial reach is unavailable or uncomfortable, do not lift: end the assigned action, have the coach manage the ball and use a separately eligible no-ball task later. This is one counted pickup opportunity, not an extra screening repetition; the 5 s envelope may be delayed for instruction. A smaller independent P2 route does not grant or revoke main throwing evidence; actual floor handling, rack fit, release and conduct must each suit the selected main task today.

**Advance / hold / stop:** Retain one listed action with the actual suitable ball/range where applicable. No added throw, fit trial or second handling attempt. A partial or failed action is counted and may require deferral; changing age is not a progression.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-05/direct floor handling and current comfort → one counted handling rehearsal or eligible unloaded/standing replacement → known E0 with its own gates. Neither P2 handling nor a first E0 release alone qualifies the new cue choice.

### E0 — Rehearse the known task within the first counted main opportunity (Explosiveness)

**Mapping:** CHEST-PASS-TEACH — proposed local known-release / go-no-go contexts; canonical profile-versus-card identity and current release remain unresolved. See prescriptions/OR09_LIBRARY_MAPPING.md. **Set purpose / contribution:** Use the first existing main opportunity to rehearse the exact known throw on an early individual THROW cue and observe current retrieval conduct. This is one of the total 3/2/2/1 opportunities, not an extra preparation release. Known-cue instruction or a separate no-release task remains available when throwing is emerging or unavailable.

**Setup and execution:** Use the actual fitting ball in both hands at chest height, palms/finger pads on its rear-side surfaces, thumbs comfortably behind and wrists comfortable. Keep elbows below shoulder height and both feet planted in the owned bilateral stance. Only the addressed athlete picks up from the fitting rack. On the early individual THROW cue, project forward with the same smooth quick easy intent, no dip, step, jump or rotation; finish balanced without a catch or chase. Wait behind the line. Before collection every action must have stopped, every actually released ball settled and every retained ball securely parked. Only released-ball owners retrieve after global all-clear, using their own unobstructed lane and ordinary controlled pickup/carry to the rack. A mixed triad need not release three balls. No-release alternatives follow their own complete action and remain behind the line or in the assigned visible bay.

**Cues:** Your lane; wait for your name and throw cue; send it forward; stay at the line; retrieve only together; carry it back. **Common errors:** Throwing on another athlete’s cue, chasing a ball before all-clear, rapid partner exchange, adding a step/dip, throwing toward people, arching, choosing a heavier ball to reach a target, or treating a known permission cue as a measured reaction test.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Resolve the outline’s familiar release rehearsal inside the main allocation with a complete release/shutdown/retrieval clock. Prior stable task evidence plus this current response protects subsequent choice learning; the first-ever E0 throw cannot create prior repeatability.

**Metadata:** Method: repeated single observed teaching attempts with complete reset. Tenets: Strength / Body Control; coordination, perception and equipment conduct integrated. Pattern: bilateral horizontal projection. Physiology: low-volume release practice; no output gain claimed. Equipment: inspected ball, rack and contained open lane.

**Competency / within-age override:** Choice-route E0 retains actual repeatable same-demand throw, handling and known-cue/retrieval behavior. For a FIRST known-cue instructional throw, require actual comfortable grip/chest setup, floor pickup/set-down and ordinary carry, comfortable unloaded arm path, understood individual permission and group retrieval rules, fitting rack access, current suitable response and physically verified containment/supervision. Prior successful throwing or a floor push-up is not required. Unknown handling stays on its separately gated no-release instruction until established. A first known-cue throw stays on the known route today; one E0 result does not supply prior repeatability for choice. E0 does not teach a choice. Stop for changed response, insecure ball, unsuitable lane/traffic or misunderstanding.

**Advance / hold / stop:** Keep the actual implement, feet, no-preload start, direction, easy intent and full recovery. Cap1 and compressed L use E0 only. A fault consumes E0; it is not replaced or ignored to access E1.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-05/direct known task → first counted E0 rehearsal today → only remaining eligible E1 opportunities; no additional rehearsal precedes them.

### E1 — Choose THROW or HOLD while retaining the owned movement (Explosiveness)

**Mapping:** CHEST-PASS-TEACH — proposed local known-release / go-no-go contexts; canonical profile-versus-card identity and current release remain unresolved. See prescriptions/OR09_LIBRARY_MAPPING.md. **Set purpose / contribution:** Each remaining opportunity teaches one early cue interpretation while preserving the actual throw and movement demand. THROW produces one owned release; HOLD retains the ball for 3 s, parks it and waits. Both are counted. Known-cue, hold, unloaded and standing routes have their separate maintenance/instruction jobs and do not earn unobserved choice results.

**Setup and execution:** Use the unchanged stationary bilateral chest start and no deliberate dip, step, jump, rotation or wall/partner target. Only the addressed athlete picks up. After readiness is confirmed, the coach says exactly THROW or HOLD once. THROW retains the owned forward release and balanced finish. HOLD keeps the same two-hand chest position and both feet planted for 3 s, then lowers to the own fitting rack over a 5 s allowance and settles behind the line. No forward push-out or fake throw. Do not preannounce the next choice, accelerate cue timing or score reaction speed. Released-ball owners alone retrieve after whole-group all-clear; everyone else stays behind the line or in the assigned visible bay.

**Cues:** Wait for your own name. Hear the whole cue. THROW: use your known throw. HOLD: keep it, park it, wait. All-clear before collection. **Common errors:** Releasing for another athlete’s cue; turning HOLD into a throw or feint; catching/chasing; a new dip or harder throw; rushing set-down; replacing a false release; counting a canceled cue as observed; restoring extra GO attempts to match old throw totals.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Early go/no-go interpretation changes one decision demand on a genuinely retained task. First known-release learning remains a separate route. A partner/variable target pass, squat-to-wall throw or directional cut would change the movement/space contract and is not selected.

**Metadata:** Method: low-density early go/no-go instruction with recovered individual permission. Tenets: Coordination, Body Control and Explosiveness on actual throws; Strength support remains separate. Perception/inhibition and equipment conduct are observed; no reaction-speed, sport-transfer or maximal-output result is claimed.

**Competency / within-age override:** For FIRST choice instruction, require actual repeatable same-implement/stance/direction/intent throwing, secure handling and whole known-release/retrieval behavior; confirm both word meanings before holding a ball. Prior successful go/no-go mastery is not required. Today’s E0 must also retain suitable response/conduct. A single first or emerging E0 throw cannot replace prior repeatability. For RETAINED choice work, use direct actual repeatable same-demand throw and cue behavior without requiring attendance at OR-05. If throw/cue prerequisites are missing, use the independently eligible known/no-release route or defer. One remaining choice observes only its presented rule; neither attendance nor successful HOLD alone proves both rules. Operational source approval is separately unverified.

**Advance / hold / stop:** Retain the total actual main opportunity ceiling, ball, stance, direction, intent and recovery. Default E0+E1 totals standard D3/L2, compressed D2/L1. E1 after E0 is respectively2/1/1/0, before smaller cap1/2. Select HOLD→THROW or THROW→HOLD and take only the remaining prefix; never add a cue to complete both rules. Reduce or end remaining opportunities if attention or movement deteriorates. Do not count NO-GO as a missing throw owed later.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-05/direct stable throw and known-release/retrieval, plus current E0 → first or retained early choice in remaining OR-09 opportunities → OR-13 uses only its independently qualified movement/cue branch → OR-17 observes the actual retained rule with reduced prompting, not faster cues.

### S1 — Retain the familiar push pattern (Strength)

**Mapping:** INCLINE-PUSH **Set purpose / contribution:** One push set retains actual setup, range and manageable effort while the cue choice receives teaching attention. Preserve recent smaller counts; it supplies the push role without another throwing or decision task.

**Setup and execution:** Use the verified high support with hands secure and feet at their recorded position. Keep an organized body line, lower2s and press1s through an owned range. The first listed rep is the observation.

**Cues:** Stable hands; one body line; controlled lower; press away. **Common errors:** Sag/pike, forced depth, unstable surface, uncounted demonstration reps or a lower support assigned solely to older athletes.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Push then pull receive first attention within Strength; knee, hip and brace follow. Five sequential whole-group roles each use 6 min standard or 4 min compressed. Familiar work needs no extra set or superset to fill its slot. Equal complete setup/recovery windows keep the optional familiar DB hip feasible without rushing the lower-body roles.

**Metadata:** Method: retained supported practice. Tenets: Strength/Body Control. Pattern: horizontal push/brace.

### S2 — Retain the familiar pull pattern (Strength)

**Mapping:** SUPPORTED-ROW **Set purpose / contribution:** One pull set retains actual setup, range and manageable effort while the cue choice receives teaching attention. Preserve recent smaller counts; it supplies the pull role without another throwing or decision task.

**Setup and execution:** Place opposite hand AND knee on the suitable bench, other foot planted. Pull1s, hold the top1s, lower2s; park and change sides in10s.10s total pickup/set-down is included. Count both sides within one set.

**Cues:** Hand and knee supported; quiet trunk; pull smoothly; lower and park. **Common errors:** Hand-only standing row, torso twist, unsuitable bench, dropping the implement or treating8total reps as8each side.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Push then pull receive first attention within Strength; knee, hip and brace follow. Five sequential whole-group roles each use 6 min standard or 4 min compressed. Familiar work needs no extra set or superset to fill its slot. Equal complete setup/recovery windows keep the optional familiar DB hip feasible without rushing the lower-body roles.

**Metadata:** Method: retained supported pull. Tenets: Strength/Coordination. Five benches and five manageable dumbbells are assumed.

### S3 — Retain the familiar knee pattern (Strength)

**Mapping:** SQUAT-BW **Set purpose / contribution:** One knee set retains actual setup, range and manageable effort while the cue choice receives teaching attention. Preserve recent smaller counts; it supplies the knee role without another throwing or decision task.

**Setup and execution:** Use a comfortable bilateral base and owned range. Lower3s, pause gently1s, stand1s. Keep whole-foot pressure and natural breathing. No box or hand support is assumed.

**Cues:** Whole foot; comfortable squat; pause softly; stand tall. **Common errors:** Forcing depth, bouncing, holding breath or treating this supporting set as a loading test.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Push then pull receive first attention within Strength; knee, hip and brace follow. Five sequential whole-group roles each use 6 min standard or 4 min compressed. Familiar work needs no extra set or superset to fill its slot. Equal complete setup/recovery windows keep the optional familiar DB hip feasible without rushing the lower-body roles.

**Metadata:** Method: retained observed practice. Tenets: Strength/Body Control. Pattern: bilateral knee. Equipment: none.

**Advance / hold / stop:** Retain the most recent compatible actual variation, support, load, range, count and response. Choose explicit smaller packets when indicated; no load, stance or support-complexity increase accompanies the first cue choice. Count a first low-demand observation within its listed set if independently suitable; unfamiliar loaded handling is deferred. Symptoms or unowned control end the affected role, without an added replacement set.

**Prior → current → next:** Actual OR-08 or more recent/direct equivalent knee record, including smaller counts and unloading → one explicit compatible knee set in OR-09 → OR-13/17 retain that actual demand while their independently eligible cue branch changes or is observed. Attendance and throwing success do not restore work.

### S4 — Retain the familiar hip pattern (Strength)

**Mapping:** HINGE-BW; independently familiar TWO-DB RDL alternative retains DB-RDL mechanics **Set purpose / contribution:** One hip set retains actual setup, range and manageable effort while the cue choice receives teaching attention. Preserve recent smaller counts; it supplies the hip role without another throwing or decision task.

**Setup and execution:** Default: arms crossed at chest, soft knees, hips back3s and stand1s through owned range,5s setup/exit. Familiar DB alternative: actual retained pair at individually fitting elevated parking positions. Hold one DB in each hand with palms inward and arms long; establish a comfortable tall top start with weights near the thighs. Hinge through the owned comfortable hamstring-limited range, with no floor-touch or forced stretch target;4s controlled pickup, three-second lowering/one-second standing repetitions,4s controlled set-down and2s clear. Soft knees, organized trunk, weights remain close; no wall touch, new reach target or grip/handling test. Record kg per DB, total load and actual parking height/range. First-time pickup/top-start/hinge/set-down instruction is not available in this loaded route.

**Cues:** Keep the owned setup. Hips back. Stand smoothly. Park both weights under control when using them. **Common errors:** Restoring old DBs after more recent unloading; assuming a firstOR06 teaching set proves familiar handling; one implement substituted for two; a new load or longer set; rushed handling or an extra trial to choose the route.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Push then pull receive first attention within Strength; knee, hip and brace follow. Five sequential whole-group roles each use 6 min standard or 4 min compressed. Familiar work needs no extra set or superset to fill its slot. Equal complete setup/recovery windows keep the optional familiar DB hip feasible without rushing the lower-body roles.

**Metadata:** Method: retained or reduced supporting hip practice. Tenets: Strength and Body Control. Bodyweight good morning or independently familiar two-dumbbell RDL; exact identity and handling remain distinct.

### S5 — Retain the familiar brace pattern (Strength)

**Mapping:** HEEL-TAP **Set purpose / contribution:** One brace set retains actual setup, range and manageable effort while the cue choice receives teaching attention. Preserve recent smaller counts; it supplies the brace role without another throwing or decision task.

**Setup and execution:** Supine on the mat, arms fixed vertical, hips/knees in comfortable tabletop. Lower one bent leg until a light heel contact at the selected target, return, alternate.2s out/2s back. Tabletop check is setup; first listed contact is the counted moving-limb observation.

**Cues:** Quiet trunk; light heel; return; keep breathing. **Common errors:** Heel slide, straight-leg reach, moving the opposite arm, forced floor depth or extra reps to test readiness.

**Selection / order / pairing:** Push then pull receive first attention within Strength; knee, hip and brace follow. Five sequential whole-group roles each use 6 min standard or 4 min compressed. Familiar work needs no extra set or superset to fill its slot. Equal complete setup/recovery windows keep the optional familiar DB hip feasible without rushing the lower-body roles.

**Metadata:** Method: observed low-load control. Tenets: Strength/Body Control. Pattern: supine brace with alternating hip motion.

## Detailed group timing

**Preparation, 180 s after the full base.** P1 uses10 s reminder then groups of five at10/20/30 s, one10 s action each. P2 uses20 s gather/demo then triads at20/44/68/92/116 s, one24 s floor-handling action each:5 s pickup,5 s chest hold,4 s carry/turn,6 s floor set-down,4 s exit. Shorter unloaded/standing choices use10 s and rest for the remainder. The last triad ends at140 s. P1 rest is at least20 s; the fixed-order P1→P2 gap is at least40 s. Actual extra fit changes are not assumed to fit; omit/delay remaining work. Standing choices remain in directly visible personal bays. Main-opening setup contains any required return from P2 to those bays.

**Main setup and the first counted rehearsal.** Main-relative0–240 s covers the actual recorded route, ball/rack/containment check, cue explanation, coach demonstration, water and grouping. No extra athlete throw or test pickup occurs. E0 is the first counted main opportunity at relative240 s, using one known THROW cue or the separately eligible no-release task. First-ever or emerging throwing stays on the known route; it cannot supply prior repeatability for E1 choice. If E0 current response is unsuitable, end or simplify remaining work under its independent gates without repayment.

**One90 s triad.** Athlete starts0/15/30 s are sequential. THROW takes10 s:2 s rack pickup/chest setup,2 s readiness/individual cue,1 s release,3 s follow-through,2 s wait; the remaining5 s slot is quiet waiting. HOLD takes15 s:2 s pickup,2 s readiness/cue,3 s chest hold,5 s own-rack set-down,3 s settle behind the line. No-release hold/reach/standing uses its explicit10 s action and quiet remaining slot. Everyone stays behind the line until the scheduled phase. At45–50 s, confirm every action stopped, all held balls parked and released balls settled; every lane remains shut. Only released-ball owners retrieve at50–65 s:up to6 m outward/6 s,3 s controlled floor pickup,up to6 m loaded return/6 s. At65–75 s, owners have5 s rack set-down and5 s rear clearance; HOLD athletes perform only empty-hand rear clearance, with no second pickup/set-down or retrieval. Stationary-bay athletes stay in their bays. At75–90 s, after outgoing owners clear, the next triad walks from the rear holding marks to its own behind-line working marks/racks along an inspected unobstructed rear path. No ball is picked up during staging and no path crosses a target lane. HOLD waiting marks must leave that feed clear. The first triad stages inside opening setup; after a round the next round’s first triad stages during the final15 s before its scheduled start. The actual path and ordinary pace must fit; otherwise delay/omit. Unsafe release, intrusion or uncontrolled ball immediately closes all lanes before any scheduled later check.

**Rounds and opportunity ceilings.** Five triads start at0/90/180/270/360 s within each450 s round. E0 starts main-relative240 s. E1 rounds start780/1320 s for standard D; standard L and compressed D have only the780 s E1 round; compressed L has no E1. This retains total3/2/2/1 opportunities including E0. Cap1 keeps E0 alone; cap2 keeps E0 plus only the first E1. E1 choice uses the selected HOLD→THROW or THROW→HOLD prefix. Never append the missing cue type. The540 s interval between corresponding round starts gives at least450 s recovery after the whole90 s triad, beyond the60 s minimum. The last standard D round clears at main-relative1770 s, session44:30, leaving30 s before Strength. Compressed D clears at relative1230 s, session30:30, leaving270 s. Empty/canceled slots remain rest. The90 s gap between round clearance and the next round contains rest and next-first-triad staging in its last15 s; no extra throw occurs. No cue type or athlete choice can extend the90 s modeled triad; actual overrun triggers delay/omission.

**Strength, five familiar roles.** Standard:push45–51,pull51–57,knee57–63,hip63–69,brace69–75. Each6 min role reserves90 s setup, with five-athlete waves at90/165/240 s. Compressed:push35–39,pull39–43,knee43–47,hip47–51,brace51–55. Each4 min role reserves60 s setup, with waves at60/120/180 s. Keep the same athlete groups and observe two/three athletes per coach. Longest reference bouts are push17 s,row52 s,knee25 s,BW hip21 s,familiar DB hip26 s,heel taps26 s; breathing is40 s plus15 s quiet recovery. DB hip has10 s handling plus at most16 s repetitions and20 s between-wave reset, fitting60 s compact pitch. The last compact DB bout ends at206 s, leaving34 s in its role; its90 s recovery fits before that athlete’s brace wave. Other demanding roles retain60 s recovery; breathing retains its separately specified15 s. No additional strength set, circuit, athlete equipment race or physical finisher is added.

## Alternatives and constraints

Select the main route before delivery from actual evidence. **Choice** keeps a repeatable throw and known-release/retrieval, confirms signals, uses known E0 then only remaining choices. **Known** teaches or retains the original single-cue throw under OR-05’s component gates, without prior whole-throw mastery; no choice is added today from one emerging E0 result. **Hold** uses actual comfortable ball handling and a fitting rack but never releases. **Reach** uses an independently comfortable empty-hand arm path. **Standing** requires independently comfortable quiet bilateral standing. If even that is unsuitable, defer. Any current source/variant approval still needs verification; these proposed local contexts are not approved substitutes. Search/resolve a current approved exact alternative before operational release rather than silently switching to a wall, partner, squat, catch or reactive target.

Main THROW mechanics retain both palms/finger pads on the rear-side ball surfaces, thumbs comfortably behind, comfortable wrists, elbows below shoulder height, no forced hand angle, chest-height start and balanced planted feet. HOLD retains these contacts, with a3 s post-cue hold and a controlled5 s rack set-down; it is not a loaded press. A failed HOLD release remains an actual throw and a consumed cue. If THROW produces no release, record that response and arrange controlled parking within remaining allowance or delay; do not issue another cue. Only actually presented cues count as observations; canceled future cues remain unobserved.

The mode table below gives complete main task packets for every age. Choose one route and one cue order. E0 is included in the total; cap1 leaves no E1, cap2 allows at most one E1. Retain any still-smaller actual record or defer; no history is invented. P2 selection is independent of prior main evidence, but current floor access, handling and response must still qualify the chosen main task. Preparation standing/unloaded choices use the complete age/mode packets below and earn only their actual task observation.

The shown default is HOLD first. THROW first uses the same dose, effort, recovery and action contracts with the first two E1 choices in the opposite order. Standard D reference observes HOLD+THROW or THROW+HOLD; standard L/compressed D reference observes only the selected first choice; compressed L and cap1 observe no E1 choice. Cap2 truncates standard D to the selected first choice. No extra cue completes a pair.

**Strength history choices.** Retain one set per role, actual setup/load/range and the most recent smaller counts. Knee/hip cap1/2/3 means the smaller of that cap and the selected explicit reference. Unknown suitable bodyweight knee/hip instruction begins at1×2, with the first counted repetition observed; no extra fit trial. Familiar DB hip is D-only and needs the actual most recent compatible pair/load/range/count and independent10 s handling. A first loaded lesson is not familiarity. More recent unloading governs over older DB work; missed attendance creates neither unloading nor load permission. One DB per hand, palms inward, long arms, tall top start near thighs, soft knees and owned comfortable hinge range; no floor-touch target. If ten fitting DBs/five stations or direct views are unavailable, choose a declared compatible BW reduction before pickup or defer. A fault after pickup ends that counted set without replacement.

For push/pull/brace, **low** retains the existing L counts even in D. Row always includes both sides and full handling/side change. Suspension replaces pull only with independently suitable secure anchors/stance and its own actual recorded inclination; low D uses the existing L3 bilateral repetitions. Supported breathing replaces brace only with its own lower-leg/arm/position gates; four comfortable2 s in/3 s out cycles, no forced depth/holds, calves/heels fully supported, no heel pull or hip lift,20 s setup and15 s quiet recovery. It does not establish moving-limb tabletop competence. Still-smaller actual history uses a recorded reduction or deferral rather than automatic reference restoration.

## Workload accounting

Count P1 unloaded reach/standing, P2 pickup/5 s hold/2 m carry/floor set-down, E0 known-cue opportunity and each E1 cue opportunity separately. Record intended THROW/HOLD distribution, actual presented cues, correct/incorrect/ambiguous responses, actual releases including faults, post-cue holds, pickups/set-downs and owner retrieval. Never use valid responses as the physical dose counter. Three/two/two/one total main opportunities include E0; HOLD can reduce intended throws without creating a debt. A single remaining choice cannot prove both rules. Standard D choice with HOLD-first has one E0 throw, one HOLD and one THROW before any faults; smaller and reverse-order prefixes are explicit.

Ordinary floor handling and ball retrieval are not running, intentional athlete jumping or landing. All prescribed main variants keep zero intentional athlete flight events; unknown actual ordinary contacts remain unknown. Retrieval planning caps are at most6 m outward plus6 m loaded return per actual released ball, with actual distance recorded rather than presumed equal to the cap. NO-GO/no-release variants have no ball-retrieval distance; stationary bays have no lane return. Fault recovery may add actual handling that must be recorded and reduces/defer remaining work instead of being ignored. Strength remains one set each of push, pull, knee, hip and brace with its selected cap and support option. Do not sum alternative routes or timing envelopes as one athlete workout. Actual load, symptoms, cue status and completed work remain null until recorded.

## Final window and separate tumbling

Standard75–90 or compressed55–60 is water/recovery, descriptive reflection, recording and transfer, with zero additional physical sets. Ask the athlete to explain the actual cue, whether the ball should have stayed or gone, and the all-clear rule; no extra athlete demonstration, penalty or throw game. Share actual shoulder/wrist/grip/trunk response, lower-body work, attention, faults and handling with the separate coach. Body Control/Tumbling remains its own30 min segment after the athletic block. Its precise approved session and exercise doses are unresolved; this handoff is not a completed tumbling prescription or advanced-skill clearance.

## Coach record and next decision

Record actual prior same-demand throw/known-cue/retrieval evidence and whether today is first choice instruction, retained choice or known/no-release learning. Enter actual ball type/kg/diameter/rebound, rack/support heights, containment and sightlines, E0 response, selected E1 cue order and opportunity cap. For each scheduled action record whether a cue was presented, which cue, actual response and releases/holds/handling; distinguish misunderstanding, distraction, fatigue and movement loss without diagnosing. Canceled cues remain unobserved. Record actual most recent knee/hip/push/pull/brace variation, load/count, newer unloading and selected reductions; unknown history is not zero or presumed completion.

Next OR-13 requires its own qualified movement branch: stationary throwing evidence does not grant a directional step, braking or reactive cut. If stable throwing and early release are separately passed while HOLD remains emerging, the main REACT-T permission may retain only that passed early-release rule under the entry matrix. OR-17 observes the actual retained cue rule with reduced prompting, not a new faster cue or harder implement. JUMP-T uses its separately qualified throwing branch without landing credit. No athlete automatically advances because OR-09 was offered or attended.
